I've completed my read-only review. Here is the design critique.

---

## Bottom line

**Ship with changes.** The core idea (a single persisted `LIVE_ROSTER` row + a negative gate that demotes everything except the roster) is sound and correctly avoids the CLAUDE.md:32 collision by using a new `signal_type`. But as designed it has one real silent-live gap (the four hand-rolled detectors), it fails open on a missing/stale roster (the dangerous direction), it omits a schema constraint that will break the daily+weekly cron, and it produces **4 live types, not 6** unless you also lift `MANUAL_SUPPRESS_OVERRIDE` (which contradicts your own `87ed29c` decision from earlier today). Details below.

---

## A. Are `computeSuppressionSets` + `getCanonicalLiveStatus` the complete choke points? **No.**

There are **13 real `INSERT INTO active_setups` sites in `server/`** (7 in `acd.js` + 6 standalone service pollers). They split into three groups:

**Group 1 — go through the setupEligibility functions, so the roster gate WOULD reach them (via `computeSuppressionSets`'s `suppressedSetups` or `getCanonicalLiveStatus`):**

- `acd.js` all 7 sites: Globex `1024` (uses `live.status` from `getOvernightLevelLiveStatus` → `getCanonicalLiveStatus`, `acd.js:367-374`), RTH `3220` (STACK_VOL_BREAK via `getStackVolBreakLiveStatus` → `getCanonicalLiveStatus`, `acd.js:386-389`), sibling `4778` (gated by `isLiveEligible`, `acd.js:4737`), audit `5298` (default SHADOW, promotes only via `suppressAllDisabledOverrides`, `acd.js:5230`), bt `6157` (`btCanPromote` via `suppressAllDisabledOverrides`, `acd.js:6149`), vol-building active `6746` (gated by `baseIneligible = !isLiveEligible(...)`, `acd.js:6628`), shadow vol-building `7088` (SHADOW by name). The `shadowCandidates` loop is gated by `isLiveEligible` at `acd.js:7007`.
- `minorDefendedLevelDetector.js:373`, `stallDefendedLevelDetector.js:383`, `majorPivotDefendedBreakDetector.js:334` — all call `getCanonicalLiveStatus` and use `.status` directly. Covered.
- `ibLowPnrDetector.js:136` and `overnightOrderflowEntryDetector.js:146` — call `getCanonicalLiveStatus` but then hardcode `status='SHADOW'` (real N=0). Covered/safe regardless.
- `momentumChaseDetector.js:143-152` — hardcoded `'SHADOW','SHADOW'` (`NO_LIVE_PATH_HARDWIRED_SHADOW`). Never live. Safe.
- `sweepReversalMerged.js` — SHADOW-only by design (header `:15-16`), only feeds `acd.js`'s `shadowCandidates`. Safe.
- `acdRiskGates.js:260` — the "INSERT" match is a comment, not a real insert site.

**Group 2 — the gap. Four detectors decide ACTIVE/SHADOW with a hand-rolled `N>=20 / EV<-5` check and NEVER consult `setupEligibility`:**

- `globexFlushDetector.js:151-174` — `getLiveStatus(setupType)` for `GLOBEX_FLUSH_LONG/SHORT/REVERSAL_*`.
- `rthFlushDetector.js:60-78` — `getLiveStatus(setupType)` for `RTH_FLUSH_LONG/SHORT`.
- `minuteBarSignalDetector.js:97-107` — `getLiveStatus()` for `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND/_BALANCE_FADE`.
- `pocRotationJoinDetector.js:85-97` — `getLiveStatus(setupType)` for `POC_ROTATION_JOIN_LONG/SHORT` (currently forced SHADOW by `POC_JOIN_LIVE.enabled=false`, `:84`).

None of these four setup types is on the 6-type roster, so under your design they **must** be shadowed — but none of them would be, because the roster gate (in `computeSuppressionSets`/`getCanonicalLiveStatus`) never reaches their `getLiveStatus`. This is precisely the `GLOBEX_PAUSED` shape from CLAUDE.md:88 ("a later override in a live-eligibility chain silently wins") and the `detectGlobexSetup()`/`globexFlushDetector.js` companion-override pair from CLAUDE.md:411.

Today it's **latent** (all four sit below N=20, or are flag-disabled, so they fire SHADOW anyway). But the moment `GLOBEX_FLUSH_*` or `RTH_FLUSH_*` crosses its own N≥20 bar, it would go live while off-roster — silently. The fix is cheap: add the roster check to `checkStandardLiveGates()` (`detectorLiveGates.js:47`), which `globexFlush`, `rthFlush`, and `minuteBar` already call (`globexFlushDetector.js:276`, `rthFlushDetector.js:209`, `minuteBarSignalDetector.js:163`), and add the same call to `pocRotationJoinDetector.js` (it currently only calls `isOppositeDirectionOpen`, `:182`).

---

## B. Fail-open vs fail-closed on missing/stale/empty roster

Your design (point 2) says "no roster / stale → gate OFF (current behavior)." **That is fail-open, and it is the wrong direction.** "Current behavior" = PROMOTE_ALL_MODE = ~240-254 ACTIVE. Failing open silently resurrects exactly the roster you are trying to retire, during precisely the outage (cron broke, row stale) when nobody is watching. The codebase's own convention is already fail-closed: `computeSuppressionSets` documents "empty knownTypes ⇒ every type reads unknown ⇒ ineligible" (`setupEligibility.js:161-165`), and `getCanonicalLiveStatus` returns `SHADOW` on a missing row (`setupEligibility.js:420`).

**Recommendation: fail-closed in all three cases, with a distinct banner reason for each:**

- **Missing row** → shadow everything, banner "LIVE_ROSTER missing — no live trading until the cron runs."
- **Stale (>10d)** → shadow everything, banner "LIVE_ROSTER stale — no live trading until refreshed."
- **Empty roster (`types: []`)** → shadow everything, banner "LIVE_ROSTER empty — 0 types passing the rule." (An empty roster is a *legitimate* result of the rule, not an error, and should also shadow-all — your instinct on this is right.)

The only real cost of fail-closed is "account goes dark if the cron breaks," but that is a *visible, de-risked* failure (banner + zero new risk), versus fail-open's *silent 240-type re-exposure*. If you want a middle ground, the banner can say "0 live types" and a human flips `LIVE_ROSTER_GATE.enabled=false` to fall back deliberately — but that fallback must be an explicit human action, not the default.

---

## C. Roster churn / hysteresis — this is the most serious statistical problem in the proposal

Your own framing is the key fact: **~247 types tested one-sided at 2.5% ⇒ ~6 false positives by chance; only 3 pass.** The "both chronological halves positive" gate roughly halves that, landing right at ~3 — i.e. **your 3 "passing" types are exactly consistent with zero real edge and pure multiple-testing noise.** Then the sibling rule doubles exposure to 6, of which 3 have no demonstrated edge at all (their own CI is allowed to be <0). So the live roster is a *de-risking filter* (241 → 6), not a *validated-profitability list* — and it must be treated as such.

Churn is dangerous only in the **promotion** direction (a type newly enters the roster and starts taking live capital). Demotion is always safe. So apply hysteresis **asymmetrically**:

- **Demotion: instant.** Leaving the roster = shadow immediately. No stickiness needed.
- **Promotion: data-derived stickiness, not a hardcoded "week."** The cleanest option that reuses what you already have: require the type to clear the rule on **two consecutive daily rosters**, OR better, recompute `dayBlockedBootstrapCI` (`rigorDiagnostics.js:245`) with the type's **single highest-PnL day dropped** and require `ci.lo` still > 0. The leave-one-day-out test is fully derived from the type's own data and directly answers "is this a lucky streak or a real edge" — a type whose whole CI rests on one day fails it. The two-consecutive-runs option is acceptable and simpler, but it is technically a hardcoded "2"; the leave-one-day-out variant is the one that honors "no static thresholds" cleanly.

---

## D. Interactions with the existing rich-ACTIVE-roster machinery

- **Cluster-sibling / `ALL_LEVELS_LIVE` / `is_cluster_primary`:** the 6 roster types are all `_FADE_` types, so `isLevelForcedLive` (`setupEligibility.js:292`) still scopes them. But their *declustered cluster siblings* (other levels in the same touch, e.g. `OR5_HIGH_FADE_*` when `IB_HIGH_FADE_*` wins) are non-roster ⇒ `isLiveEligible` false ⇒ SHADOW. That's the intended outcome, and it costs no data (sibling rows still INSERT as SHADOW, `acd.js:4778`, and still resolve). Just know you're shrinking the declustered *ACTIVE* sample to the 6 — that's the point.
- **`isOppositeDirectionOpen`:** direction-only and roster-wide; its population shrinks to the 6 but the logic is unchanged. No break.
- **PROMOTE_ALL_MODE's own stat gate:** the roster is an **additional negative filter layered on SETUP_STATUS**, and the two can legitimately disagree. A type can be ACTIVE in SETUP_STATUS but not on the roster (the ~240 demoted) — intended. A type can be on the roster but SUPPRESS in SETUP_STATUS (the 2 manual siblings) — see E. Document the composition explicitly (live ⇔ on-roster AND not-SUPPRESS/THIN_N AND not-DOW AND not-`CAPITAL_EXPOSURE_OVERRIDE`).
- **One trap you must avoid:** do **not** fold non-roster types into `acd.js`'s `_trueSuppressedSetups` (`acd.js:4172-4174`). That's a *separate* local set the early-touch backfill loop uses to distinguish SUPPRESS from THIN_N; polluting it would silently stop backfilling THIN_N types. Put the roster only into `computeSuppressionSets`'s returned `suppressedSetups`, not into `knownTypes` and not into `_trueSuppressedSetups`.
- **ML veto gate / `closeOnRotationFlip`:** ML veto is force-SHADOW-only and orthogonal (a roster type can still be ML-vetoed per fire — fine). `closeOnRotationFlip` is exit-side and setup_type-agnostic — no interaction.
- **Two side effects to audit** because `computeSuppressionSets` has two other consumers beyond `acd.js`:
  1. `server/services/sierraChart/orderEligibility.js:68` calls `computeSuppressionSets` directly. That means the roster gate **automatically blocks real Sim1 DTC orders for non-roster types** — exactly what you want ("no real Sim1 orders") — *but* its rejection reason (`:70`) would mislabel a roster demotion as "SETUP_STATUS suppresses … SUPPRESS or THIN_N." Add a distinct roster reason there.
  2. `server/services/levelApproach.js:60` calls `computeSuppressionSets(0)`. This is a backtest population filter; adding non-roster types to `suppressedSetups` changes its input population. Confirm that's acceptable.
- **`SUPPRESS_ALL_DISABLED` short-circuit:** `isLiveEligible` (`setupEligibility.js:377`) and `getCanonicalLiveStatus` (`:417`) both return ACTIVE *before* consulting `suppressedSetups` when that flag is on. If you add the roster only inside `computeSuppressionSets`, a future `SUPPRESS_ALL_DISABLED` re-enable would silently bypass the roster. Put the roster check **where `CAPITAL_EXPOSURE_OVERRIDE` is already checked** (inside those same two branches), so it survives the flag — same reason `orderEligibility.js:7-16` deliberately uses `computeSuppressionSets` and not the flag-honoring functions.

---

## E. The `MANUAL_SUPPRESS_OVERRIDE` conflict — leave it in force (⇒ 4 live, not 6)

`scripts/backtest_setup_status.mjs:250-261` is unambiguous: earlier today (`87ed29c`) you explicitly moved `IB_LOW_FADE_SHORT` (CI [-59,-9], entirely below zero), `PD_IB_MID_FADE_LONG`, and `IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG` (CI [-26,+3], mean negative) to SHADOW, with the comment "the family survives, only its losing direction moves to SHADOW." **That commit *is* the "exclude a sibling whose own CI is below zero" refinement you now say to ignore.** The two instructions are in direct conflict for exactly the two roster siblings (`IB_LOW_FADE_SHORT`, `IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG`).

Two coherent resolutions, pick explicitly:

1. **Leave `MANUAL_SUPPRESS_OVERRIDE` in force (recommended).** The roster is a *negative* gate (it selects the candidate pool, it does not certify profitability). SETUP_STATUS + MANUAL_SUPPRESS remain the real EV gates. Result:
   - `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`, `IB_LOW_FADE_LONG`, `OR30_LOW_FADE_LONG`, `OR30_LOW_FADE_SHORT` → live (**4**).
   - `IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG` → SHADOW (manual, mean negative).
   - `IB_LOW_FADE_SHORT` → SHADOW (manual **and** its CI is entirely below zero, so PROMOTE_ALL_MODE's own stat gate would suppress it anyway — lifting the manual entry alone wouldn't make it live).
   
   "The family survives" is honored at the **selection** level (all 6 are the only allowed candidates), but real capital is still withheld from the two whose own data is negative. This is consistent with the "unified suppression pipeline" hard rule and with your own prior decision.

2. **Literally 6 live.** That requires the roster to become a **positive allowlist that bypasses SETUP_STATUS** for its members, plus lifting both manual entries. This is a hardcoded 6-type allowlist that overrides a confirmed negative-EV type — precisely what you said the six are *not* ("not a hand list to hardcode"), and precisely the negative-EV-fires-live failure `MANUAL_SUPPRESS_OVERRIDE` exists to stop. I'd advise against it.

Whatever you choose: **record it in the `LIVE_ROSTER` row's `notes`** (per-type: `passing` vs `sibling` vs `also_suppressed_by_manual/stat_gate`), so the 6-vs-4 gap is visible, not silent.

---

## F. Hard-rule issues and concrete changes

1. **`performance_audit` unique constraint breaks your "one row per run."** `schema.sql:10921` has `UNIQUE (run_date, window_days, signal_type, signal_name)`, and `window_days` is `NOT NULL` with no default (`schema.sql:6213`). Your design never mentions `window_days`, and a daily + weekly cron running on the same day with `signal_name='CURRENT'` would collide on `(run_date, window_days, signal_type, signal_name)`. Fix: supply a real `window_days` (e.g. the lookback the rule consumed), and use `ON CONFLICT (run_date, window_days, signal_type, signal_name) DO UPDATE`, or make the daily/weekly run converge intentionally. This is a hard, concrete break, not a style nit.
2. **Fail-open is the design's one direct conflict with the codebase's fail-closed convention** (see B).
3. **The four hand-rolled `getLiveStatus` detectors** are a live "later override silently wins" gap (CLAUDE.md:88) that the design's "(a)+(b)" placement does not close (see A).
4. **Filter drift:** "same filters as family_preview" is correct, but do **not** reach for `REAL_TRADE_FILTER` (`backtest_setup_status.mjs:82`) as a shortcut — it additionally excludes `resolution_method IN ('MARK_TO_MARKET','RECOVERY_MTM')`, which `family_preview_20261021.mjs:5` does *not* (it only excludes `NOT_FILLED`). Moot for the 6 fade types (fixed stop/target exits), but a silent divergence if you generalize. Also: the design adds "POC_ROTATION_JOIN excluded," which `family_preview` does **not** have. Resolve that inconsistency — either export `family_preview`'s filter as a shared constant, or document the POC exclusion as a deliberate extra (it's redundant today: POC's CI crosses zero and `POC_JOIN_LIVE.enabled=false` already forces it SHADOW).
5. **"10 days old" staleness** is a hand-picked operational constant. It does not violate "no static thresholds" (that rule is about trading cutoffs, CLAUDE.md:24), but justify it against the cron cadence (e.g., "10 days = 2 missed weekly runs ⇒ treat as down") rather than leaving it unstated.
6. **`signal_type='LIVE_ROSTER'` is the right collision avoidance** (CLAUDE.md:32) — a new distinct `signal_type`, never `SETUP_STATUS`/`OPTIMAL_STOP`. Good. Just also satisfy the reader-side `DISTINCT ON (signal_name) ... ORDER BY signal_name, run_date DESC, id DESC` (CLAUDE.md:30) exactly, which your design already states.

---

## Verdict

**Ship with these changes, in priority order:** (1) fail-closed on missing/stale/empty roster with a distinct banner reason; (2) wire the roster check into `checkStandardLiveGates()` so `globexFlush`/`rthFlush`/`minuteBar` (and `pocRotationJoin`) can't silently go live off-roster; (3) fix the `performance_audit` `window_days`/unique-constraint/ON-CONFLICT detail; (4) place the roster check alongside `CAPITAL_EXPOSURE_OVERRIDE` so `SUPPRESS_ALL_DISABLED` can't bypass it; (5) leave `MANUAL_SUPPRESS_OVERRIDE` in force and document that the effective roster is 4 live + 2 shadow (surface it, don't silently ship "6"); (6) add promotion-only hysteresis (two consecutive daily rosters or a leave-one-day-out CI); (7) reuse `family_preview`'s exact filter (not `REAL_TRADE_FILTER`) and reconcile the POC exclusion; (8) add a distinct roster reason in `orderEligibility.js` and audit `levelApproach.js`.
